        }));
      });
    
      it('traces successful reads with workspace, actor, parameters, result, and latency', async () => {

    diff --git a/backend/src/agent/services/context.service.js b/backend/src/agent/services/context.service.js

    +const { ForbiddenError } = require('../../utils/errors');
    +
    +/**
    + * Context is supplied by server code after requireAuth/loadDbUser/
    + * requireMembership. It must never be built from model arguments.
    + * Shape: { userId: req.user.id, member: req.member, workspace: req.workspace }.
    + */
    +function assertAgentContext(context, minRole = 'member') {
    +  const member = context?.member;
    +  const workspace = context?.workspace;
    +  const hasId = (id) => typeof id === 'string' && id.trim().length > 0;
    +
    +  if (!hasId(context?.userId) || !hasId(member?.id) || !hasId(workspace?.id)
    +      || member.workspaceId !== workspace.id || member.isActive !== true
    +      || !['member', 'admin'].includes(member.role)) {
    +    throw new ForbiddenError('Active workspace membership required');
    +  }
    +
    +  if (minRole === 'admin' && member.role !== 'admin') {
    +    throw new ForbiddenError('Admin access required');
    +  }
    +
    +  // Unknown permission requirements must never grant access.
    +  if (!['member', 'admin'].includes(minRole)) {
    +    throw new ForbiddenError('Tool access denied');
    +  }
    +}
    +
    +module.exports = { assertAgentContext };
    diff --git a/backend/src/agent/services/read-tools.service.js b/backend/src/agent/services/read-tools.service.js

    +++ b/backend/src/agent/services/read-tools.service.js
    @@ -0,0 +1,35 @@
    +const dashboardService = require('../../services/dashboard.service');
    +const { assertAgentContext } = require('./context.service');
    +
    +async function getMyContext({ context }) {
    +  assertAgentContext(context);
    +  const { member, workspace } = context;
    +
    +  // Whitelist context fields; never return the full middleware objects.
    +  return {
    +    member_id: member.id,
    +    role: member.role,
    +    display_name: member.displayName,
    +    is_proxy: member.isProxy,
    +    workspace_id: workspace.id,
    +    workspace_name: workspace.name,
    +    base_currency: workspace.baseCurrency,
    +  };
    +}
    +
    +async function getDashboard({ context }) {
    +  assertAgentContext(context);
    +  return dashboardService.getDashboardData({
    +    workspaceId: context.workspace.id,
    +    isAdmin: context.member.role === 'admin',
    +    memberId: context.member.id,
    +  });
    +}
    +
    +async function getOverdueSummary({ context }) {
    +  // The existing service relies on its HTTP route's admin gate.
    +  assertAgentContext(context, 'admin');
    +  return dashboardService.getOverdueSummaryData({ workspaceId: context.workspace.id });
    +}
    +
    +module.exports = { getMyContext, getDashboard, getOverdueSummary };
    diff --git a/backend/src/agent/services/tool-registry.service.js b/backend/src/agent/services/tool-registry.service.js
    new file mode 100644
    index 0000000..165d9c1
    --- /dev/null
    +++ b/backend/src/agent/services/tool-registry.service.js
    @@ -0,0 +1,89 @@
    +const { z } = require('zod');
    +const { ValidationError, NotFoundError, BusinessRuleError } = require('../../utils/errors');
    +const logger = require('../../utils/logger');
    +const { assertAgentContext } = require('./context.service');
    +const { getMyContext, getDashboard, getOverdueSummary } = require('./read-tools.service');
    +
    +const tools = Object.freeze([
    +  Object.freeze({
    +    name: 'get_my_context',
    +    description: 'Get your identity, role, and current workspace.',
    +    parameterSchema: z.object({}).strict(),
    +    minRole: 'member',
    +    tier: 0,
    +    handler: getMyContext,
    +  }),
    +  Object.freeze({
    +    name: 'get_dashboard',
    +    description: 'Get the current workspace dashboard with your permitted view and personal task summary.',
    +    parameterSchema: z.object({}).strict(),
    +    minRole: 'member',
    +    tier: 1,
    +    handler: getDashboard,
    +  }),
    +  Object.freeze({
    +    name: 'get_overdue_summary',
    +    description: 'Get the workspace overdue contribution summary. Admin access required.',
    +    parameterSchema: z.object({}).strict(),
    +    minRole: 'admin',
    +    tier: 1,
    +    handler: getOverdueSummary,
    +  }),
    +]);
    +
    +/** Return role-filtered definitions; executable handlers stay private. */
    +function getAvailableTools(context) {
    +  assertAgentContext(context);
    +  return Object.freeze(tools
    +    .filter((tool) => tool.minRole === 'member' || context.member.role === 'admin')
    +    .map(({ handler, ...definition }) => Object.freeze(definition)));
    +}
    +
    +/**
    + * Execute validated reads only. A future proposal executor must be a
    + * separate entry point; no model-controlled flag can enable writes here.
    + * Results remain service data, including any untrusted user-generated text.
    + */
    +async function executeReadTool({ name, args = {}, context }) {
    +  const startedAt = Date.now();
    +  const tool = tools.find((candidate) => candidate.name === name);
    +  const trace = {
    +    toolName: typeof name === 'string' ? name : null,
    +    tier: tool?.tier,
    +    workspaceId: context?.workspace?.id,
    +    actorUserId: context?.userId,
    +    actorMemberId: context?.member?.id,
    +  };
    +
    +  try {
    +    assertAgentContext(context);
    +    if (!tool) throw new NotFoundError('Agent tool not found');
    +    assertAgentContext(context, tool.minRole);
    +    if (tool.tier > 1) {
    +      throw new BusinessRuleError('Write tools require an approved proposal');
    +    }
    +
    +    const parsed = tool.parameterSchema.safeParse(args);
    +    if (!parsed.success) {
    +      throw new ValidationError('Invalid tool arguments', null, parsed.error.issues);
    +    }
    +
    +    trace.parameters = parsed.data;
    +    const result = await tool.handler({ context, args: parsed.data });
    +    logger.info('Agent read tool completed', {
    +      ...trace, result, durationMs: Date.now() - startedAt, outcome: 'success',
    +    });
    +    return result;
    +  } catch (err) {
    +    logger.warn('Agent read tool failed', {
    +      ...trace,
    +      error: err.message,
    +      errorCode: err.code,
    +      durationMs: Date.now() - startedAt,
    +      outcome: 'error',
    +    });
    +    throw err;
    +  }
    +}
    +
    +module.exports = { getAvailableTools, executeReadTool };

    // src/services/search.service.js
    //
    // Query-building/escaping/shaping logic for cross-entity search. The
    // controller is a thin HTTP adapter around this.
    
    const { supabaseAdmin } = require('../config/supabase');
    const { containsPattern } = require('../utils/ilike');
    
    /**
     * Cross-entity search across active members (by display_name) and
     * containers (by name) in parallel. Results are typed so the client can
     * render them differently. Scoped to the current workspace.
     *
     * ILIKE wildcard escaping is shared via utils/ilike.js.
     *
     * @returns {Promise<{results: Array, query: string}>}
     */
    async function searchWorkspace({ workspaceId, query, limit = 10 }) {
      const trimmedQuery = (query || '').trim();
      const safeLimit    = Math.min(20, parseInt(limit) || 10);
    
      if (!trimmedQuery || trimmedQuery.length < 2) {
        return { results: [], query: trimmedQuery };
      }
    
      const pattern = containsPattern(trimmedQuery);
    
      const [{ data: members }, { data: containers }] = await Promise.all([
        supabaseAdmin
          .from('workspace_members')
          .select('id, display_name, role, is_proxy')
          .eq('workspace_id', workspaceId)
          .eq('is_active', true)
          .is('deleted_at', null)
          .ilike('display_name', pattern)
          .limit(safeLimit),
    
        supabaseAdmin
          .from('containers')
          .select('id, name, container_type, status')
          .eq('workspace_id', workspaceId)
          .is('deleted_at', null)
          .ilike('name', pattern)
          .limit(safeLimit),
      ]);
    
      const results = [
        ...(members    || []).map((m) => ({ type: 'member',    id: m.id, display_name: m.display_name, role: m.role, is_proxy: m.is_proxy })),
        ...(containers || []).map((c) => ({ type: 'container', id: c.id, name: c.name, container_type: c.container_type, status: c.status })),
      ];
    
      return { results, query: trimmedQuery };
    }
    
    module.exports = { searchWorkspace };
    // tests/unit/services/search.service.test.js
    jest.mock('../../../src/config/supabase', () => {
      const { mockSupabase: createMock } = require('../../mocks/supabase.mock');
      const instance = createMock();
      return {
        supabaseAdmin: instance.client,
        supabase: instance.client,
        supabaseAuth: instance.client,
        __mockInstance: instance,
      };
    });
    
    const { searchWorkspace } = require('../../../src/services/search.service');
    const mockSupabaseInstance = require('../../../src/config/supabase').__mockInstance;
    
    const WORKSPACE_ID = 'ws-1';
    
    describe('services/search.service — searchWorkspace', () => {
      beforeEach(() => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.reset();
      });
    
      it('empty query -> returns { results: [], query } WITHOUT hitting the DB', async () => {
        const result = await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: '' });
    
        expect(result).toEqual({ results: [], query: '' });
        expect(mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().length).toBe(0);
      });
    
      it('1-character query -> returns empty results without hitting the DB (below the 2-char minimum)', async () => {
        const result = await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: 'a' });
    
        expect(result.results).toEqual([]);
        expect(mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().length).toBe(0);
      });
    
      it('whitespace-only query is trimmed and treated as empty', async () => {
        const result = await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: '   ' });
    
        expect(result).toEqual({ results: [], query: '' });
      });
    
      it('2-character query (boundary) DOES hit the DB', async () => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
    
        await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: 'bo' });
    
        expect(mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().length).toBeGreaterThan(0);
      });
    
      it('escapes ILIKE special characters in the query before searching', async () => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
    
        await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: '50%_x' });
    
        const ilikeCalls = mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().filter((c) => c.method === 'ilike');
        ilikeCalls.forEach((c) => expect(c.args[1]).toBe('%50\\%\\_x%'));
      });
    
      it('combines member and container results, correctly typed', async () => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [{ id: 'm1', display_name: 'Bob', role: 'member', is_proxy: false }], error: null });
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [{ id: 'c1', name: 'Bob\'s Party', container_type: 'event', status: 'active' }], error: null });
    
        const result = await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: 'bob' });
    
        expect(result.results).toEqual([
          { type: 'member', id: 'm1', display_name: 'Bob', role: 'member', is_proxy: false },
          { type: 'container', id: 'c1', name: "Bob's Party", container_type: 'event', status: 'active' },
        ]);
      });
    
      it('clamps limit to a maximum of 20', async () => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
    
        await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: 'bob', limit: 500 });
    
        const limitCalls = mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().filter((c) => c.method === 'limit');
        limitCalls.forEach((c) => expect(c.args[0]).toBe(20));
      });
    
      it('defaults limit to 10 when omitted', async () => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
    
        await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: 'bob' });
    
        const limitCalls = mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().filter((c) => c.method === 'limit');
        limitCalls.forEach((c) => expect(c.args[0]).toBe(10));
      });
    
      it('non-numeric limit falls back to the default of 10', async () => {
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
        mockSupabaseInstance.mockNextResponse({ data: [], error: null });
    
        await searchWorkspace({ workspaceId: WORKSPACE_ID, query: 'bob', limit: 'abc' });
    
        const limitCalls = mockSupabaseInstance.getCalls().filter((c) => c.method === 'limit');
        limitCalls.forEach((c) => expect(c.args[0]).toBe(10));
      });
    });

    -- =====================================================================
    -- Kith — Database Schema
    -- A shared workspace for families/groups to coordinate money pooling,
    -- recurring contributions, tasks, milestones, and notifications.
    -- =====================================================================
    
    CREATE SCHEMA IF NOT EXISTS public;
    
    SET default_tablespace = '';
    SET default_table_access_method = heap;
    
    -- Generic trigger function: keeps `updated_at` current on row update.
    -- Attached to every table below that has an `updated_at` column.
    CREATE FUNCTION public.update_updated_at_column() RETURNS trigger
        LANGUAGE plpgsql
        AS $$
    BEGIN
      NEW.updated_at = NOW();
      RETURN NEW;
    END;
    $$;
    
    -- Trigger function: blocks UPDATE/DELETE on audit_log to keep it append-only.
    -- Attached to public.audit_log below.
    CREATE FUNCTION public.audit_log_append_only() RETURNS trigger
        LANGUAGE plpgsql
        AS $$
    BEGIN
      RAISE EXCEPTION 'audit_log is append-only';
    END;
    $$;
    
    -- =====================================================================
    -- CORE: users, workspaces, membership
    -- =====================================================================
    
    CREATE TABLE public.users (
        id uuid NOT NULL,
        email text NOT NULL,
        full_name text,
        bio text,
        country_of_residence text,
        timezone text DEFAULT 'UTC'::text,
        preferred_language text DEFAULT 'en'::text,
        avatar_url text,
        auth_provider text DEFAULT 'email'::text NOT NULL,
        push_token text,
        push_token_platform text,
        push_enabled boolean DEFAULT false NOT NULL,
        email_digest_enabled boolean DEFAULT true NOT NULL,
        created_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        updated_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        last_seen_at timestamp with time zone,
        deleted_at timestamp with time zone,
        CONSTRAINT users_auth_provider_check CHECK ((auth_provider = ANY (ARRAY['email'::text, 'google'::text, 'apple'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT users_push_token_platform_check CHECK ((push_token_platform = ANY (ARRAY['web'::text, 'ios'::text, 'android'::text, NULL::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT users_pkey PRIMARY KEY (id),
        CONSTRAINT users_email_key UNIQUE (email)
    );
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_users_email ON public.users USING btree (email);
    
    CREATE TRIGGER users_updated_at BEFORE UPDATE ON public.users FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.update_updated_at_column();
    
    CREATE TABLE public.user_contacts (
        id uuid DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() NOT NULL,
        user_id uuid NOT NULL,
        type text NOT NULL,
        label text,
        value text NOT NULL,
        country_code text,
        is_primary boolean DEFAULT false NOT NULL,
        is_verified boolean DEFAULT false NOT NULL,
        verified_at timestamp with time zone,
        created_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        CONSTRAINT user_contacts_type_check CHECK ((type = ANY (ARRAY['email_secondary'::text, 'whatsapp'::text, 'phone'::text, 'telegram'::text, 'signal'::text, 'instagram'::text, 'facebook'::text, 'twitter'::text, 'linkedin'::text, 'custom'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT user_contacts_pkey PRIMARY KEY (id),
        CONSTRAINT user_contacts_user_id_type_value_key UNIQUE (user_id, type, value),
        CONSTRAINT user_contacts_user_id_fkey FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES public.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
    );
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_user_contacts_user ON public.user_contacts USING btree (user_id);
    
    CREATE TABLE public.workspaces (
        id uuid DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() NOT NULL,
        name text NOT NULL,
        base_currency text DEFAULT 'GBP'::text NOT NULL,
        family_type text DEFAULT 'extended'::text NOT NULL,
        description text,
        avatar_url text,
        bank_details jsonb DEFAULT '{}'::jsonb,
        visibility text DEFAULT 'private'::text NOT NULL,
        created_by uuid NOT NULL,
        created_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        updated_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        deleted_at timestamp with time zone,
        CONSTRAINT workspaces_family_type_check CHECK ((family_type = ANY (ARRAY['nuclear'::text, 'extended'::text, 'blended'::text, 'community'::text, 'association'::text, 'other'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT workspaces_visibility_check CHECK ((visibility = ANY (ARRAY['private'::text, 'public'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT workspaces_pkey PRIMARY KEY (id),
        CONSTRAINT workspaces_created_by_fkey FOREIGN KEY (created_by) REFERENCES public.users(id)
    );
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_workspaces_created_by ON public.workspaces USING btree (created_by);
    
    CREATE TRIGGER workspaces_updated_at BEFORE UPDATE ON public.workspaces FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.update_updated_at_column();
    
    CREATE TABLE public.workspace_members (
        id uuid DEFAULT gen_random_uuid() NOT NULL,
        workspace_id uuid NOT NULL,
        user_id uuid,
        role text DEFAULT 'member'::text NOT NULL,
        display_name text NOT NULL,
        relationship_to_head text,
        relationship_category text DEFAULT 'other'::text,
        date_of_birth date,
        is_proxy boolean DEFAULT false NOT NULL,
        proxy_managed_by uuid,
        admin_notes text,
        invite_status text,
        invited_at timestamp with time zone,
        joined_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        last_active_at timestamp with time zone,
        contribution_streak_months integer DEFAULT 0,
        last_contribution_date date,
        is_active boolean DEFAULT true NOT NULL,
        created_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        updated_at timestamp with time zone DEFAULT now() NOT NULL,
        deleted_at timestamp with time zone,
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_check CHECK ((((is_proxy = true) AND (user_id IS NULL)) OR (is_proxy = false))),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_check1 CHECK (((user_id IS NULL) OR (invite_status = 'accepted'::text) OR (invite_status IS NULL))),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_invite_status_check CHECK ((invite_status = ANY (ARRAY[NULL::text, 'pending'::text, 'accepted'::text, 'declined'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_relationship_category_check CHECK ((relationship_category = ANY (ARRAY['blood'::text, 'marriage'::text, 'in_law'::text, 'friend'::text, 'other'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_role_check CHECK ((role = ANY (ARRAY['admin'::text, 'member'::text]))),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_pkey PRIMARY KEY (id),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_workspace_id_user_id_key UNIQUE (workspace_id, user_id),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_proxy_managed_by_fkey FOREIGN KEY (proxy_managed_by) REFERENCES public.workspace_members(id),
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_user_id_fkey FOREIGN KEY (user_id) REFERENCES public.users(id) ON DELETE SET NULL,
        CONSTRAINT workspace_members_workspace_id_fkey FOREIGN KEY (workspace_id) REFERENCES public.workspaces(id) ON DELETE CASCADE
    );
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_workspace_members_proxy ON public.workspace_members USING btree (workspace_id, is_proxy);
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_workspace_members_role ON public.workspace_members USING btree (workspace_id, role);
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_workspace_members_user ON public.workspace_members USING btree (user_id) WHERE (user_id IS NOT NULL);
    
    CREATE INDEX idx_workspace_members_workspace ON public.workspace_members USING btree (workspace_id) WHERE (deleted_at IS NULL);
    
    CREATE TRIGGER workspace_members_updated_at BEFORE UPDATE ON public.workspace_members FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.update_updated_at_column();

    // tests/mocks/supabase.mock.js
    //
    // The single most important shared test utility in this project (see
    // Doc 4 Section 2.5's header note). Given how many service functions
    // make 3–6 SEQUENTIAL Supabase calls (list-then-detail, fetch-then-
    // update, parallel Promise.all fan-outs), every service unit test needs
    // to script "call 1 returns X, call 2 returns Y, call 3 errors" without
    // hand-rolling that sequencing per test file.
    //
    // USAGE
    // -----
    //   const { mockSupabase } = require('../../mocks/supabase.mock');
    //
    //   jest.mock('../../../src/config/supabase', () => {
    //     const { mockSupabase } = require('../../mocks/supabase.mock');
    //     const instance = mockSupabase();
    //     global.__supabaseMock = instance; // expose for the test file to script
    //     return { supabaseAdmin: instance.client, supabase: instance.client, supabaseAuth: instance.client };
    //   });
    //
    //   // in a test:
    //   const { mockNextResponse } = global.__supabaseMock;
    //   mockNextResponse({ data: { id: 'abc' }, error: null });      // response to call #1
    //   mockNextResponse({ data: [{ id: 'x' }], error: null, count: 1 }); // response to call #2
    //   const result = await someService.doThing({ ... });
    //
    // Prefer the exported `createSupabaseMock()` helper below (used by
    // tests/mocks/index.js and every service unit test in this package) over
    // constructing this by hand — it wires the jest.mock() call for you.
    //
    // DESIGN NOTES
    // ------------
    // - Every query-builder method returns `builder` (chainable), matching
    //   supabase-js's own fluent API.
    // - `.single()` / `.maybeSingle()` and awaiting the builder directly
    //   (`await query`, via `.then`) all pull from the SAME sequential
    //   `responses` queue, advanced by a single shared `callIndex`. This
    //   matches how supabase-js queries actually resolve — a query is a
    //   thenable, and `.single()`/`.maybeSingle()` are just the terminal
    //   call that makes it await-able with a different unwrap shape — so
    //   scripting responses in the order your service code issues queries
    //   is the correct mental model regardless of which terminal method it
    //   uses.
    // - `.rpc()` is tracked SEPARATELY from the main response queue (its own
    //   `rpcResponses` queue) since RPC calls don't go through
    //   `.from()`/`.select()` chaining at all in supabase-js — they're a
    //   distinct top-level method on the client. Script RPC responses with
    //   `mockNextRpcResponse()`.
    // - `.storage.from(bucket)` is also modeled separately (see
    //   `storageBuilder`) since storage.service.js calls
    //   `supabase.storage.from(BUCKET).createSignedUploadUrl(...)` /
    //   `.download(...)` / `.remove(...)` / `.createSignedUrl(...)`, a
    //   completely different chain shape from the Postgres query builder.
    // - `.auth` methods used directly on the client (auth.getUser,
    //   auth.admin.signOut, auth.admin.updateUserById,
    //   auth.admin.getUserById, auth.signInWithPassword, auth.signUp, etc.)
    //   are separately mockable jest.fn()s on `client.auth` / `client.auth.admin`
    //   — script them directly, e.g. `client.auth.getUser.mockResolvedValue(...)`.
    
    function createChainableBuilder(state) {
      const builder = {};
    
      const chainMethods = [
        'from', 'select', 'insert', 'update', 'delete', 'upsert',
        'eq', 'neq', 'in', 'is', 'not', 'gte', 'lte', 'lt', 'gt',
        'order', 'range', 'limit', 'ilike', 'like', 'or', 'filter',
        'contains', 'textSearch',
      ];
    
      for (const method of chainMethods) {
        builder[method] = jest.fn((...args) => {
          // `.delete({ count: 'exact' })` and similar option-passing calls
          // are recorded but otherwise behave like any other chain link.
          state.calls.push({ method, args });
          return builder;
        });
      }
    
      function nextResponse() {
        const resp = state.responses[state.callIndex] ?? { data: null, error: null, count: 0 };
        state.callIndex += 1;
        return resp;
      }
    
      builder.single = jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve(nextResponse()));
      builder.maybeSingle = jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve(nextResponse()));
    
      // Makes `await builder` and `await builder.select(...)` etc. work
      // directly (no terminal .single()/.maybeSingle()/.then() call needed),
      // exactly like a real supabase-js PostgrestFilterBuilder, which is a
      // thenable.
      builder.then = (resolve, reject) => {
        return Promise.resolve(nextResponse()).then(resolve, reject);
      };
      builder.catch = (reject) => builder.then(undefined, reject);
    
      return builder;
    }
    
    function createStorageBuilder(state) {
      return {
        from: jest.fn(() => ({
          createSignedUploadUrl: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve(state.storageResponses[state.storageCallIndex++] ?? { data: { signedUrl: 'https://storage.test/signed-upload' }, error: null })),
          createSignedUrl:       jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve(state.storageResponses[state.storageCallIndex++] ?? { data: { signedUrl: 'https://storage.test/signed-download' }, error: null })),
          download:               jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve(state.storageResponses[state.storageCallIndex++] ?? { data: { arrayBuffer: () => Promise.resolve(new ArrayBuffer(0)) }, error: null })),
          remove:                 jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve(state.storageResponses[state.storageCallIndex++] ?? { data: [{}], error: null })),
        })),
      };
    }
    
    /**
     * Creates a fresh mock Supabase client instance. Call once per test file
     * (typically inside a jest.mock() factory), and use the returned control
     * methods inside individual tests / beforeEach blocks to script
     * responses and make assertions.
     */
    function mockSupabase() {
      const state = {
        calls: [],
        responses: [],
        callIndex: 0,
        rpcCalls: [],
        rpcResponses: [],
        rpcCallIndex: 0,
        storageResponses: [],
        storageCallIndex: 0,
      };
    
      const builder = createChainableBuilder(state);
      const storage = createStorageBuilder(state);
    
      const client = {
        from: jest.fn((table) => {
          state.calls.push({ method: 'from', args: [table] });
          return builder;
        }),
        rpc: jest.fn((fnName, params) => {
          state.rpcCalls.push({ fnName, params });
          const resp = state.rpcResponses[state.rpcCallIndex] ?? { data: null, error: null };
          state.rpcCallIndex += 1;
          return Promise.resolve(resp);
        }),
        storage,
        auth: {
          getUser: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: { user: null }, error: null })),
          signInWithPassword: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
          signUp: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
          refreshSession: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
          resetPasswordForEmail: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
          verifyOtp: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
          exchangeCodeForSession: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
          admin: {
            signOut: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ error: null })),
            updateUserById: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: {}, error: null })),
            getUserById: jest.fn(() => Promise.resolve({ data: { user: null }, error: null })),
          },
        },
      };
    
      return {
        client,
        builder,
    
        /** Push the response for the NEXT sequential .single()/.maybeSingle()/await call. */
        mockNextResponse: (resp) => state.responses.push(resp),
    
        /** Push several responses at once, in call order. */
        mockResponses: (...resps) => state.responses.push(...resps),
    
        /** Push the response for the NEXT .rpc() call. */
        mockNextRpcResponse: (resp) => state.rpcResponses.push(resp),
    
        /** Push the response for the NEXT .storage.from(...).<method>() call. */
        mockNextStorageResponse: (resp) => state.storageResponses.push(resp),
    
        /** Introspection for assertions. */
        getCalls: () => state.calls,
        getRpcCalls: () => state.rpcCalls,
    
        /** Reset all scripted responses and call logs (call in afterEach if not using a fresh instance per test). */
        reset: () => {
          state.calls = [];
          state.responses = [];
          state.callIndex = 0;
          state.rpcCalls = [];
          state.rpcResponses = [];
          state.rpcCallIndex = 0;
          state.storageResponses = [];
          state.storageCallIndex = 0;
          jest.clearAllMocks();
        },
      };
    }
    
    module.exports = { mockSupabase };

    AGENTS.md
    # AGENTS.md — Kith
    
    This file orients any coding agent (Codex CLI included) working in this repo. Read it before touching code.
    
    ## What this repo is
    
    Kith is a family coordination platform: shared workspaces, a financial ledger, recurring payment pools with auto-generated billing cycles, tasks, disputes, and an audit trail — including proxy membership for non-users (kids, elderly relatives).
    
    - `backend/` — Express/Node API, Supabase (Postgres + Auth + Storage), Redis/BullMQ for background jobs.
    - `frontend/` — React/Vite/TypeScript.
    - `migrations/001_schema.sql` — the entire schema in one consolidated file. There is no incremental migration runner; this file is the single source of truth for tables, RPCs, and triggers.
    
    ## Current work: AI agent feature
    
    We are building a chat + voice AI agent that lets users ask about and act on their workspace in natural language ("how many contributions do we have", "create a container", "add this member"). It is being built **inside `backend/src/agent/`**, as a new module alongside the existing `controllers/`, `services/`, `routes/`, etc.
    
    The full design is in `KITH_AGENT_ARCHITECTURE.md` at the repo root. Read it before writing agent code — it covers the tool catalog, tiering, confirmation model, and known gaps. This AGENTS.md is about *how* to work, that doc is about *what* to build.
    
    ## How we work together
    
    **Build this interactively, not as one scaffold.** Do not generate the whole `backend/src/agent/` module in a single pass. Propose a small next step, explain the plan briefly, implement it, and stop for review before moving to the next piece. I will give you direction turn by turn on sequencing, scope, and priorities — do not assume you know the full build order in advance.
    
    When a decision isn't settled by the architecture doc or this file, ask rather than guessing silently — especially anything touching authorization, money, or what gets exposed to the model as a tool.
    
    ## Non-negotiable constraints
    
    These come from how the existing codebase actually works, not preference:
    
    1. **No database-level safety net.** Every query goes through `supabaseAdmin`, the Supabase service-role client, which bypasses Row-Level Security entirely. The service layer's manual role/ownership checks (`isAdmin`, `callerId` comparisons) are the *only* authorization boundary in this system. Any new agent tool handler must perform its own explicit role check before calling a service function — never assume the service enforces it.
    
    2. **Two existing services trust their caller completely and must not be wrapped carelessly:**
       - `services/participant.service.js` — no internal role checks at all.
       - `services/ledger.service.js#confirmEntry` — relies on the route's `requireAdmin` gate, not an internal check.
       Any tool wrapping either must add its own role check in the handler.
    
    3. **Agent tools wrap existing service functions. They never query the database directly.** This preserves field whitelisting, audit logging, notification orchestration, and cache invalidation that's already built into each service.
    
    4. **Every write is tiered** (see architecture doc §4–5): reads run freely; low-risk writes get a lightweight approval card; standard admin writes get a full approval card showing exact before/after state; sensitive actions (dispute resolution, corrections, deletions, acting on behalf of another member) require an explicit secondary confirmation. **The model's text output alone must never trigger a write** — execution only happens through a separate confirm/approve step.
    
    5. **Tool results are untrusted data, not instructions.** Ledger notes, task titles, dispute text, etc. can contain adversarial content. Never let tool output be treated as a system/control instruction.
    
    6. **The agent never does arithmetic.** Every number shown to the user comes verbatim from a tool result (e.g. `getLedgerSummary`, `getDashboardData`), never computed by the model.
    
    7. **Never build a tool around `exportLedger`, `exportTasks`, or `exportAuditLogCsv`**, or anything that produces bulk data egress, auth/password flows, workspace deletion, or role-escalation. These are explicitly out of scope for the agent (architecture doc §4, Tier 5).
    
    8. **Audit everything agent-initiated.** Use `services/audit.service.js#log()`. New action strings go into the frozen `AUDIT_ACTIONS` object in `constants/audit-actions.js`, following its existing `domain.action` naming convention — don't invent ad hoc strings.
    
    9. **Reuse existing infrastructure instead of rebuilding it:** the Redis-backed rate limiter pattern in `middleware/rateLimiter.js`, the idempotency-key pattern on `ledger_entries`, the dedup-key pattern on `notifications`, and the existing fuzzy search in `services/search.service.js`.
    
    ## Code conventions already in use (match these)
    
    - Controllers are thin: parse request → call service → shape response → `next(err)` on failure. All logic lives in `services/`.
    - Services take plain parameter objects (`{ workspaceId, data, actorCtx }`), not `req`/`res`. This is exactly what makes them callable from a tool handler as well as a controller.
    - Validation via Zod schemas in `validators/`.
    - Errors via the typed classes in `utils/errors.js` (`NotFoundError`, `BusinessRuleError`, `ValidationError`, etc.) — not raw `throw new Error(...)` in service code.
    - Structured logging via `utils/logger.js` (Winston), not `console.log`.
    - `audit.fromReq(req)` for actor context on every audited write.
    
    ## Testing
    
    - `cd backend && npm run test:unit` — runs without external services, should stay green.
    - Integration tests need a local Postgres + Redis stack (not available in this sandbox) — note when a change needs integration coverage rather than trying to fake it.
    - New agent tool handlers should get unit tests for: the permission-check branch (role denied), the happy path, and ambiguous-resolution handling where relevant.
    
    ## What not to do
    
    - Don't modify `migrations/001_schema.sql` casually — schema changes here are consolidated, not incremental; flag if the agent work needs a schema change and we'll decide together.
    - Don't add new top-level dependencies without flagging it first.
    - Don't touch `frontend/` as part of this work unless explicitly asked — this phase is backend-only.

    $ Get-Content backend/src/agent/services/tool-registry.service.js -Encoding utf8; Get-Content backend/src/services/search.service.js -Encoding utf8; Get-Content backend/src/utils/ilike.js -Encoding utf8; Get-Content backend/src/utils/errors.js -Encoding utf8
    const { z } = require('zod');
    const { ValidationError, NotFoundError, BusinessRuleError } = require('../../utils/errors');
    const logger = require('../../utils/logger');
    const { assertAgentContext } = require('./context.service');
    const { getMyContext, getDashboard, getOverdueSummary } = require('./read-tools.service');
    
    const tools = Object.freeze([
      Object.freeze({
        name: 'get_my_context',
        description: 'Get your identity, role, and current workspace.',
        parameterSchema: z.object({}).strict(),
        minRole: 'member',
        tier: 0,
        handler: getMyContext,
      }),
      Object.freeze({
        name: 'get_dashboard',
        description: 'Get the current workspace dashboard with your permitted view and personal task summary.',
        parameterSchema: z.object({}).strict(),
        minRole: 'member',
        tier: 1,
        handler: getDashboard,
      }),
      Object.freeze({
        name: 'get_overdue_summary',
        description: 'Get the workspace overdue contribution summary. Admin access required.',
        parameterSchema: z.object({}).strict(),
        minRole: 'admin',
        tier: 1,
        handler: getOverdueSummary,
      }),
    ]);
    
    /** Return role-filtered definitions; executable handlers stay private. */
    function getAvailableTools(context) {
      assertAgentContext(context);
      return Object.freeze(tools
        .filter((tool) => tool.minRole === 'member' || context.member.role === 'admin')
        .map(({ handler, ...definition }) => Object.freeze(definition)));
    }
    
    /**
     * Execute validated reads only. A future proposal executor must be a
     * separate entry point; no model-controlled flag can enable writes here.
     * Results remain service data, including any untrusted user-generated text.
     */
    async function executeReadTool({ name, args = {}, context }) {
      const startedAt = Date.now();
      const tool = tools.find((candidate) => candidate.name === name);
      const trace = {
        toolName: typeof name === 'string' ? name : null,
        tier: tool?.tier,
        workspaceId: context?.workspace?.id,
        actorUserId: context?.userId,
        actorMemberId: context?.member?.id,
      };
    
      try {
        assertAgentContext(context);
        if (!tool) throw new NotFoundError('Agent tool not found');
        assertAgentContext(context, tool.minRole);
        if (tool.tier > 1) {
          throw new BusinessRuleError('Write tools require an approved proposal');
        }
    
        const parsed = tool.parameterSchema.safeParse(args);
        if (!parsed.success) {
          throw new ValidationError('Invalid tool arguments', null, parsed.error.issues);
        }
    
        trace.parameters = parsed.data;
        const result = await tool.handler({ context, args: parsed.data });
        logger.info('Agent read tool completed', {
          ...trace, result, durationMs: Date.now() - startedAt, outcome: 'success',
        });
        return result;
      } catch (err) {
        logger.warn('Agent read tool failed', {
          ...trace,
          error: err.message,
          errorCode: err.code,
          durationMs: Date.now() - startedAt,
          outcome: 'error',
        });
        throw err;
      }
    }
    
    module.exports = { getAvailableTools, executeReadTool };
    // src/services/search.service.js
    //
    // Query-building/escaping/shaping logic for cross-entity search. The
    // controller is a thin HTTP adapter around this.
    
    const { supabaseAdmin } = require('../config/supabase');
    const { containsPattern } = require('../utils/ilike');
    
    /**
     * Cross-entity search across active members (by display_name) and
     * containers (by name) in parallel. Results are typed so the client can
     * render them differently. Scoped to the current workspace.
     *
     * ILIKE wildcard escaping is shared via utils/ilike.js.
     *
     * @returns {Promise<{results: Array, query: string}>}
     */
    async function searchWorkspace({ workspaceId, query, limit = 10 }) {
      const trimmedQuery = (query || '').trim();
      const safeLimit    = Math.min(20, parseInt(limit) || 10);
    
      if (!trimmedQuery || trimmedQuery.length < 2) {
        return { results: [], query: trimmedQuery };
      }
    
      const pattern = containsPattern(trimmedQuery);
    
      const [{ data: members }, { data: containers }] = await Promise.all([
        supabaseAdmin
          .from('workspace_members')
          .select('id, display_name, role, is_proxy')
          .eq('workspace_id', workspaceId)
          .eq('is_active', true)
          .is('deleted_at', null)
          .ilike('display_name', pattern)
          .limit(safeLimit),
    
        supabaseAdmin
          .from('containers')
          .select('id, name, container_type, status')
          .eq('workspace_id', workspaceId)
          .is('deleted_at', null)
          .ilike('name', pattern)
          .limit(safeLimit),
      ]);
    
      const results = [
        ...(members    || []).map((m) => ({ type: 'member',    id: m.id, display_name: m.display_name, role: m.role, is_proxy: m.is_proxy })),
        ...(containers || []).map((c) => ({ type: 'container', id: c.id, name: c.name, container_type: c.container_type, status: c.status })),
      ];
    
      return { results, query: trimmedQuery };
    }
    
    module.exports = { searchWorkspace };
    // src/utils/ilike.js
    //
    // `%` and `_` are ILIKE wildcard metacharacters. Interpolating raw user
    // input into a `%...%` pattern lets a user's own `%`/`_`/`\` characters
    // silently change the matching behavior (broader or narrower than
    // intended). Escape before building the pattern.
    
    function escapeIlike(str) {
      return String(str ?? '').replace(/[%_\\]/g, (ch) => `\\${ch}`);
    }
    
    /** Builds a safe `%...%` "contains" ILIKE pattern from raw user input. */
    function containsPattern(str) {
      return `%${escapeIlike(str)}%`;
    }
    
    module.exports = { escapeIlike, containsPattern };
    // src/utils/errors.js
    
    class AppError extends Error {
      constructor(message, statusCode, code, details = null) {
        super(message);
        this.name = 'AppError';
        this.statusCode = statusCode;
        this.code = code;
        this.details = details;
        Error.captureStackTrace(this, this.constructor);
      }
    }
    
    class ValidationError extends AppError {
      constructor(message, field = null, details = null) {
        super(message, 400, 'VALIDATION_FAILED', details);
        this.field = field;
      }
    }
    
    class UnauthorizedError extends AppError {
      constructor(message = 'Authentication required') {
        super(message, 401, 'UNAUTHORIZED');
      }
    }
    
    class ForbiddenError extends AppError {
      constructor(message = 'Access denied') {
        super(message, 403, 'FORBIDDEN');
      }
    }
    
    class NotFoundError extends AppError {
      constructor(message = 'Resource not found') {
        super(message, 404, 'NOT_FOUND');
      }
    }
    
    class ConflictError extends AppError {
      constructor(message, details = null) {
        super(message, 409, 'CONFLICT', details);
      }
    }
    
    class BusinessRuleError extends AppError {
      constructor(message, details = null) {
        super(message, 422, 'BUSINESS_RULE_VIOLATION', details);
      }
    }
    
    class RateLimitError extends AppError {
      constructor(message = 'Too many requests') {
        super(message, 429, 'RATE_LIMITED');
      }
    }
    
    module.exports = {
      AppError,
      ValidationError,
      UnauthorizedError,
      ForbiddenError,
      NotFoundError,
      ConflictError,
      BusinessRuleError,
      RateLimitError,
    };

    17:This fact drives the single most important design rule for the agent: **the agent must never be given a path to any service function that skips this boundary.** Every tool handler is responsible for its own authorization check, explicitly, before calling a service function — even when the service function trusts its caller (several do; see §4.3).
    42:## 3. Search & Entity Resolution Layer
    46:### 3.1 Existing foundation
    49:### 3.2 Required extensions
    50:- **Trigram matching.** Add a `pg_trgm` extension and GIN trigram indexes on `workspace_members.display_name` and `containers.name` so fuzzy matches survive typos and voice-transcription errors (e.g. "Adaze" → "Adaeze"). Plain ILIKE `%...%` alone is too brittle for voice input.
    56:- **Ranking and disambiguation contract.** Every resolution tool returns a ranked list of candidates with their IDs, never a single guessed answer. If there's one strong match, the agent proceeds with a brief confirmation ("I found Adaeze Okafor — is that who you mean?"). If there are multiple plausible matches, the agent must present the choices and let the user pick. **The agent must never execute a write against an ambiguously-resolved ID.** This is the single highest-value safety rule for this layer — a misresolved contributor on a money entry is a direct financial error.

          displayName: 'Adaeze',

          name: 'Family',
          baseCurrency: 'NGN',
          visibility: 'private',
        },
      };
    }
    
    describe('agent read-tool foundation', () => {

          .toEqual(['get_my_context', 'get_dashboard']);

        const available = getAvailableTools(makeContext());
        expect(() => available.pop()).toThrow(TypeError);
        expect(Reflect.set(available[0], 'name', 'delete_workspace')).toBe(false);
        expect(getAvailableTools(makeContext())[0].name).toBe('get_my_context');
      });
    
      it('returns only whitelisted context fields from the authenticated workspace', async () => {

    CREATE FUNCTION public.raise_dispute_atomic(p_workspace_id uuid, p_ledger_entry_id uuid, p_raised_by uuid, p_reason text) RETURNS jsonb
        LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER
        SET search_path TO 'public'
        AS $$
        DECLARE
          v_entry   public.ledger_entries;
            v_dispute public.disputes;
            BEGIN
              SELECT * INTO v_entry FROM public.ledger_entries
                 WHERE id = p_ledger_entry_id AND workspace_id = p_workspace_id
                    FOR UPDATE;
    
                      IF v_entry.id IS NULL THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'ENTRY_NOT_FOUND'; END IF;
                        IF v_entry.status NOT IN ('confirmed', 'pending') THEN RAISE EXCEPTION 'ENTRY_NOT_DISPUTABLE'; END IF;
                          IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.disputes WHERE ledger_entry_id = p_ledger_entry_id AND status = 'open') THEN
                              RAISE EXCEPTION 'DISPUTE_ALREADY_OPEN';
                                END IF;
    
                                  INSERT INTO public.disputes (workspace_id, ledger_entry_id, raised_by, reason, status)
                                    VALUES (p_workspace_id, p_ledger_entry_id, p_raised_by, p_reason, 'open')
                                      RETURNING * INTO v_dispute;
    
                                        UPDATE public.ledger_entries SET status = 'disputed' WHERE id = p_ledger_entry_id;
    
                                          RETURN to_jsonb(v_dispute);
                                          END;
                                          $$;
    
    CREATE FUNCTION public.resolve_dispute_atomic(p_dispute_id uuid, p_resolution_note text, p_resolved_by uuid, p_resolved_at timestamp with time zone) RETURNS jsonb
        LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER
        AS $$
    DECLARE
        v_dispute public.disputes;
    BEGIN
        UPDATE public.disputes
        SET status          = 'resolved',
            resolution_note = p_resolution_note,
            resolved_by     = p_resolved_by,
            resolved_at     = p_resolved_at
        WHERE id = p_dispute_id
          AND status = 'open'
        RETURNING * INTO v_dispute;
    
        IF v_dispute.id IS NULL THEN
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'Dispute % not found or already resolved', p_dispute_id;
        END IF;
    
        UPDATE public.ledger_entries
        SET status = 'resolved'
        WHERE id = v_dispute.ledger_entry_id
          AND status = 'disputed';
    
        RETURN to_jsonb(v_dispute);
    EXCEPTION
        WHEN OTHERS THEN
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'Failed to resolve dispute: %', SQLERRM;
    END;
    $$;
    
    CREATE FUNCTION public.replace_user_contacts_atomic(p_user_id uuid, p_types text[], p_contacts jsonb) RETURNS jsonb
        LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER
        AS $$
    DECLARE
        v_result jsonb;
    BEGIN
        DELETE FROM public.user_contacts
        WHERE user_id = p_user_id
          AND type = ANY(p_types);
    
        INSERT INTO public.user_contacts (
            user_id, type, value, label, country_code, is_primary
        )
        SELECT
            p_user_id,
            (c->>'type')::text,
            (c->>'value')::text,
            NULLIF(c->>'label', ''),
            NULLIF(c->>'country_code', ''),
            COALESCE((c->>'is_primary')::boolean, false)
        FROM jsonb_array_elements(p_contacts) AS c;
    
        SELECT COALESCE(jsonb_agg(to_jsonb(uc)), '[]'::jsonb)
        INTO v_result
        FROM public.user_contacts uc
        WHERE uc.user_id = p_user_id
          AND uc.type = ANY(p_types);
    
        RETURN v_result;
    EXCEPTION
        WHEN OTHERS THEN
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'Failed to replace user contacts: %', SQLERRM;
    END;
    $$;
    
    CREATE FUNCTION public.upsert_primary_contact(p_user_id uuid, p_type text, p_value text, p_label text, p_country_code text, p_is_primary boolean) RETURNS jsonb
        LANGUAGE plpgsql SECURITY DEFINER
        AS $$
    DECLARE
        v_contact public.user_contacts;
    BEGIN
        IF p_is_primary THEN
            UPDATE public.user_contacts
            SET is_primary = false
            WHERE user_id = p_user_id
              AND type = p_type
              AND value <> p_value;
        END IF;
    
        INSERT INTO public.user_contacts (
            user_id, type, value, label, country_code, is_primary
        )
        VALUES (
            p_user_id, p_type, p_value, p_label, p_country_code, p_is_primary
        )
        ON CONFLICT (user_id, type, value)
        DO UPDATE SET
            label        = EXCLUDED.label,
            country_code = EXCLUDED.country_code,
            is_primary   = EXCLUDED.is_primary
        RETURNING * INTO v_contact;
    
        RETURN to_jsonb(v_contact);
    EXCEPTION
        WHEN OTHERS THEN
            RAISE EXCEPTION 'Failed to upsert contact: %', SQLERRM;
    END;
    $$;

        .from('users')

        .select()
        .single();
      if (uErr) throw new Error(`seedAdditionalMember: user insert failed: ${uErr.message}`);
    
      const { data: member, error: mErr } = await supabaseAdmin
        .from('workspace_members')
        .insert(buildMember({ workspace_id: workspaceId, user_id: user.id, role: 'member', ...overrides.member }))
        .select()
        .single();
      if (mErr) throw new Error(`seedAdditionalMember: member insert failed: ${mErr.message}`);

    /**
     * Seeds a container in an existing workspace, with an optional set of
     * participants (each { workspace_member_id, ...participantOverrides }).
     */
    async function seedContainerWithParticipants(supabaseAdmin, { workspaceId, createdBy, containerOverrides = {}, participants = [] }) {
      const { data: container, error: cErr } = await supabaseAdmin
        .from('containers')
        .insert(buildContainer({ workspace_id: workspaceId, created_by: createdBy, ...containerOverrides }))
        .select()
        .single();
      if (cErr) throw new Error(`seedContainerWithParticipants: container insert failed: ${cErr.message}`);
    
      const seededParticipants = [];
      for (const p of participants) {
        const { data: participant, error: pErr } = await supabaseAdmin
          .from('container_participants')
          .insert(buildParticipant({ container_id: container.id, ...p }))
          .select()
          .single();
        if (pErr) throw new Error(`seedContainerWithParticipants: participant insert failed: ${pErr.message}`);
        seededParticipants.push(participant);
      }
    
      return { container, participants: seededParticipants };
    }

    async function seedLedgerEntry(supabaseAdmin, overrides = {}) {

        .insert(buildLedgerEntry(overrides))
        .select()
        .single();
      if (error) throw new Error(`seedLedgerEntry: insert failed: ${error.message}`);

    async function seedGroupWithMembers(supabaseAdmin, { workspaceId, createdBy, groupOverrides = {}, memberIds = [] }) {

        .from('groups')
        .insert(buildGroup({ workspace_id: workspaceId, created_by: createdBy, ...groupOverrides }))
        .select()
        .single();
      if (gErr) throw new Error(`seedGroupWithMembers: group insert failed: ${gErr.message}`);
    
      if (memberIds.length) {
        const rows = memberIds.map((id) => ({ group_id: group.id, workspace_member_id: id, added_by: createdBy }));
        const { error: gmErr } = await supabaseAdmin.from('group_members').insert(rows);
        if (gmErr) throw new Error(`seedGroupWithMembers: group_members insert failed: ${gmErr.message}`);
      }
    
      return group;
    }

    /**
     * Hard-deletes everything created for a workspace, in FK-safe order.
     * Use in an afterEach/afterAll for integration test files that seed a
     * lot of relational data and want a clean slate without waiting for a
     * full DB reset between files.
     *
     * NOTE: relies on ON DELETE CASCADE for most child tables (see
     * schema.txt's FK constraints on containers/groups/milestones/
     * invite_links/workspace_members, all of which cascade from
     * workspace_id) — deleting the workspace row itself is usually
     * sufficient. ledger_entries/disputes do NOT cascade from workspace_id
     * in the schema (no ON DELETE clause), so they're cleaned up explicitly
     * first to avoid FK violations on the workspace delete.
     */
    // ledger_entries (no deleting confirmed rows) and audit_log (append-only)
    // are protected by BEFORE DELETE triggers, so PostgREST deletes of them
    // always fail with P0001 and then the workspace delete fails its FK
    // (23503), leaving every seeded workspace behind. Test-only escape hatch:
    // a direct connection with session_replication_role = replica, which
    // skips triggers (and FK enforcement) for that one transaction. The
    // triggers stay in force for the app and for every other test path.
    // Only the protected/non-cascading tables are removed this way; the
    // workspace itself is then deleted normally so ON DELETE CASCADE runs.
    let _pgPool = null;
    function getPgPool() {
      if (!_pgPool) {
        const { Pool } = require('pg');
        _pgPool = new Pool({
          connectionString: process.env.TEST_DATABASE_URL
            || 'postgres://postgres:test@localhost:54322/kith_test', // docker-compose.test.yml / CI
          max: 2,
          idleTimeoutMillis: 1000,
          allowExitOnIdle: true,
        });
      }
      return _pgPool;
    }
    
    // Tables with no workspace_id column of their own, keyed through a parent
    // that has one. Must be removed BEFORE the parent rows (the subqueries
    // need the parents to still exist). With FK enforcement off in replica
    // mode, order only matters for these subqueries.
    const CHILD_TABLE_PURGES = [
      ['cycle_carry_forwards',    'container_id', 'containers'],
      ['contributor_targets',     'container_id', 'containers'],
      ['container_cycles',        'container_id', 'containers'],
      ['container_participants',  'container_id', 'containers'],
      ['container_tasks',         'container_id', 'containers'],
      ['pool_cycle_overrides',    'container_id', 'containers'],
      ['group_members',           'group_id',     'groups'],
      ['member_profile_audit',    'workspace_member_id', 'workspace_members'],
      ['notification_deliveries', 'notification_id', 'notifications'],
    ];
    
    async function purgeProtectedRows(workspaceId) {
      const client = await getPgPool().connect();
      try {
        await client.query('BEGIN');
        // Skips triggers AND FK enforcement/cascades for this transaction only,
        // so the whole workspace is removed explicitly here instead of relying
        // on ON DELETE CASCADE ordering (which also trips on non-cascading
        // FKs into workspace_members, e.g. container_participants).
        await client.query("SET LOCAL session_replication_role = 'replica'");
    
        for (const [table, col, parent] of CHILD_TABLE_PURGES) {
          await client.query(
            `DELETE FROM public.${table} WHERE ${col} IN (SELECT id FROM public.${parent} WHERE workspace_id = $1)`,
            [workspaceId]
          );
        }
    
        const { rows } = await client.query(
          `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.columns
            WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND column_name = 'workspace_id'
              AND table_name <> 'workspaces'
              AND table_name IN (SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables
                                  WHERE table_schema = 'public' AND table_type = 'BASE TABLE')`
        );
        for (const { table_name } of rows) {
          await client.query(`DELETE FROM public."${table_name}" WHERE workspace_id = $1`, [workspaceId]);
        }
    
        await client.query('DELETE FROM public.workspaces WHERE id = $1', [workspaceId]);
        await client.query('COMMIT');
        return true;
      } catch (err) {
        await client.query('ROLLBACK').catch(() => {});
        if (!purgeProtectedRows.warned) {
          purgeProtectedRows.warned = true;
          console.warn(`cleanupWorkspace: direct purge unavailable (${err.message}); set TEST_DATABASE_URL. Falling back to PostgREST deletes.`);
        }
        return false;
      } finally {
        client.release();
      }
    }
    
    async function cleanupWorkspace(supabaseAdmin, workspaceId) {
      const purged = await purgeProtectedRows(workspaceId);
    
      if (!purged) {
        // Fallback (no direct DB access): best effort via PostgREST.
        const { error: e1 } = await supabaseAdmin.from('ledger_entries').delete().eq('workspace_id', workspaceId);
        if (e1) console.error('cleanupWorkspace: ledger_entries error:', e1);
        const { error: e2 } = await supabaseAdmin.from('disputes').delete().eq('workspace_id', workspaceId);
        if (e2) console.error('cleanupWorkspace: disputes error:', e2);
        const { error: e4 } = await supabaseAdmin.from('audit_log').delete().eq('workspace_id', workspaceId);
        if (e4) console.error('cleanupWorkspace: audit_log error:', e4);
      }
    
      const { error: e3 } = await supabaseAdmin.from('notifications').delete().eq('workspace_id', workspaceId);
      if (e3) console.error('cleanupWorkspace: notifications error:', e3);
    
      const { error: e5 } = await supabaseAdmin.from('workspace_settings').delete().eq('workspace_id', workspaceId);
      if (e5) console.error('cleanupWorkspace: workspace_settings error:', e5);
    
      const { error: e6 } = await supabaseAdmin.from('workspaces').delete().eq('id', workspaceId);
      if (e6) console.error('cleanupWorkspace: workspaces error:', e6);
    }
    
    module.exports = {
      seedWorkspaceWithAdmin,
      seedAdditionalMember,
      seedContainerWithParticipants,
      seedLedgerEntry,
      seedGroupWithMembers,
      seedMilestone,
      seedInviteLink,
      cleanupWorkspace,
    };
    # docker-compose.test.yml
    #
    # Brings up an isolated Postgres + PostgREST + Redis for integration
    # tests. Ports are deliberately non-default (54322 / 3001 / 63790) so
    # this can run alongside a developer's normal local Postgres/Redis
    # without colliding.
    #
    # IMPORTANT — why PostgREST is here: the app talks to Postgres through
    # @supabase/supabase-js, which is a REST client — it calls an HTTP API
    # (PostgREST), not the Postgres wire protocol directly. A bare Postgres
    # container alone (the original docker-compose.test.yml as drafted) has
    # no REST endpoint for supabase-js to hit, so SUPABASE_URL must point at
    # PostgREST (port 3001 below), not at Postgres's own port (54322).
    # postgres-test remains useful for direct psql access (e.g. applying
    # migrations, ad hoc debugging).
    #
    # Usage:
    #   docker compose -f docker-compose.test.yml up -d
    #   # then apply migrations/001_schema.sql to postgres-test (see README.md)
    #   npm run test:integration
    #   docker compose -f docker-compose.test.yml down -v
    
    services:
      postgres-test:
        image: postgres:16-alpine
        environment:
          POSTGRES_PASSWORD: test
          POSTGRES_DB: kith_test
        ports:
          - "54322:5432"
        healthcheck:
          test: ["CMD-SHELL", "pg_isready -U postgres"]
          interval: 5s
          timeout: 5s
          retries: 10
    
      postgrest-test:
        image: postgrest/postgrest:v12.0.2
        depends_on:
          postgres-test:
            condition: service_healthy
        environment:
          PGRST_DB_URI: postgres://postgres:test@postgres-test:5432/kith_test
          PGRST_DB_SCHEMAS: public
          PGRST_DB_ANON_ROLE: postgres
          # Test-only: using the postgres superuser as the anon/service role
          # is NOT safe for production, but is the simplest way to get
          # supabase-js's admin client working against a disposable local
          # test database without also standing up GoTrue.
          PGRST_JWT_SECRET: ${TEST_JWT_SECRET:-test-jwt-secret-at-least-32-characters-long}
        ports:
          - "3001:3000"
    
      redis-test:
        image: redis:7-alpine
        ports:
          - "63790:6379"
        healthcheck:
          test: ["CMD", "redis-cli", "ping"]
          interval: 5s
          timeout: 5s
          retries: 10

    const { seedWorkspaceWithAdmin, seedAdditionalMember, cleanupWorkspace } = require('../../helpers/dbHelper');
    
    describe('group.routes.js + workspace-invites.routes.js', () => {
      const app = getTestApp();
      const seededWorkspaceIds = [];
    
      afterAll(async () => {
        for (const id of seededWorkspaceIds) {
          await cleanupWorkspace(supabaseAdmin, id).catch(() => {});
        }
      });
    
      async function seedTenant() {
        const { workspace, user, member } = await seedWorkspaceWithAdmin(supabaseAdmin);
        seededWorkspaceIds.push(workspace.id);
        const { authHeader } = mockAuthenticatedRequest({ supabaseAdmin, userId: user.id, email: user.email });
        return { workspace, adminMember: member, adminAuthHeader: authHeader };
      }
    
      async function seedNonAdmin(workspaceId) {
        const { user, member } = await seedAdditionalMember(supabaseAdmin, workspaceId, { member: { role: 'member' } });
        const { authHeader } = mockAuthenticatedRequest({ supabaseAdmin, userId: user.id, email: user.email });
        return { user, member, authHeader };
      }
    
      // ── POST /groups ────────────────────────────────────────────────
      describe('POST /v1/workspaces/:workspaceId/groups', () => {
        it('initial member_ids with one invalid/foreign ID is silently skipped; valid ones are added', async () => {
          const { workspace, adminAuthHeader } = await seedTenant();
          const { member: validMember } = await seedNonAdmin(workspace.id);
    
          const res = await request(app)
            .post(`/v1/workspaces/${workspace.id}/groups`)
            .set('Authorization', adminAuthHeader)
            .send({ name: 'The Cousins', member_ids: [validMember.id, require('crypto').randomUUID()] });
    
          expect(res.status).toBe(201);
    
          const { data: groupMembers } = await supabaseAdmin
            .from('group_members').select('*').eq('group_id', res.body.data.group.id);
          expect(groupMembers.length).toBe(1);
          expect(groupMembers[0].workspace_member_id).toBe(validMember.id);
        });
    
        it('non-admin caller is rejected with 403', async () => {
          const { workspace } = await seedTenant();
          const { authHeader } = await seedNonAdmin(workspace.id);
    
          const res = await request(app)
            .post(`/v1/workspaces/${workspace.id}/groups`)
            .set('Authorization', authHeader)
            .send({ name: 'Should not be allowed' });
    
          expect(res.status).toBe(403);
        });
      });

          const res = await request(app)

          expect(res.status).toBe(200);
          expect(res.body.data.added_count).toBe(0);

          const { count } = await supabaseAdmin
            .from('group_members').select('*', { count: 'exact', head: true }).eq('group_id', group.id).eq('workspace_member_id', member.id);

Exact names will rank first, followed by substring and typo matches. Multiple candidates will always be marked ambiguous, and a single candidate will still need confirmation before a future write.

The new RPCs will use the workspace from server context and be callable only by the backend database role. I’ll also fetch an extra result so the result limit cannot hide ambiguity.

    -module.exports = { searchWorkspace };
    +/**
    + * Shared adapter for the ranked member/container RPCs. All results are
    + * candidates: single_match still needs user confirmation before a write.
    + * Scores are database similarities, not probabilities or proof of identity.
    + */
    +async function findCandidates(params, rpcName, shapeCandidate) {
    +  const parsed = entitySearchRequestSchema.safeParse(params);
    +  if (!parsed.success) {
    +    throw new ValidationError('Invalid entity search', null, parsed.error.issues);
    +  }
    +  const { workspaceId, query, limit } = parsed.data;
    +
    +  let rows;
    +  try {
    +    const { data, error } = await supabaseAdmin.rpc(rpcName, {
    +      p_workspace_id: workspaceId,
    +      p_query: query,
    +      p_pattern: containsPattern(query),
    +      p_limit: limit + 1,
    +    });
    +    if (error || !Array.isArray(data)) {
    +      logger.error('Ranked entity search failed', { workspaceId, rpcName, error: error?.message || 'Invalid RPC result' });
    +      throw new AppError('Entity search is unavailable. Please try again.', 503, 'SEARCH_UNAVAILABLE');
    +    }
    +    rows = data;
    +  } catch (err) {
    +    if (err instanceof AppError) throw err;
    +    logger.error('Ranked entity search failed', { workspaceId, rpcName, error: err.message });
    +    throw new AppError('Entity search is unavailable. Please try again.', 503, 'SEARCH_UNAVAILABLE');
    +  }
    +
    +  // Determine ambiguity before truncation. Never select the highest-scoring
    +  // candidate on behalf of the caller, even if it is an exact match.
    +  return {
    +    query,
    +    status: rows.length === 0 ? 'not_found' : rows.length === 1 ? 'single_match' : 'ambiguous',
    +    candidates: rows.slice(0, limit).map(shapeCandidate),
    +    has_more: rows.length > limit,
    +  };
    +}
    +
    +async function findMembers(params) {
    +  return findCandidates(params, 'search_members_ranked', (row) => ({
    +    id: row.id, display_name: row.display_name, role: row.role, is_proxy: row.is_proxy,
    +    match_type: row.match_type, score: row.score,
    +  }));
    +}
    +
    +async function findContainers(params) {
    +  return findCandidates(params, 'search_containers_ranked', (row) => ({
    +    id: row.id, name: row.name, container_type: row.container_type, status: row.status,
    +    match_type: row.match_type, score: row.score,
    +  }));
    +}
    +
    +module.exports = { searchWorkspace, findMembers, findContainers };

    const { z } = require('zod');
    
    // At least two choices can be displayed when the query is ambiguous.
    const entitySearchSchema = z.object({
      query: z.string().trim().min(2).max(200),
      limit: z.number().int().min(2).max(20).optional().default(10),
    }).strict();
    
    const entitySearchRequestSchema = entitySearchSchema.extend({
      workspaceId: z.string().uuid(),
    });
    
    module.exports = { entitySearchSchema, entitySearchRequestSchema };

    +-- Ranked entity resolution. search.service supplies p_pattern using the shared
    +-- containsPattern() escaping helper and requests one extra row for has_more.
    +-- The %> operator is word_similarity(p_query, name): it can match a misspelled
    +-- first name inside a full name, and is supported by the GIN trigram indexes.
    +-- Pin the threshold per call so connection/session settings cannot change it.
    +CREATE FUNCTION public.search_members_ranked(
    +    p_workspace_id uuid, p_query text, p_pattern text, p_limit integer DEFAULT 11
    +) RETURNS TABLE (id uuid, display_name text, role text, is_proxy boolean, match_type text, score real)
    +    LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY INVOKER
    +    SET search_path TO pg_catalog, public, extensions, pg_temp
    +    SET pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold TO '0.3'
    +    AS $$
    +    WITH matches AS (
    +        SELECT m.id, m.display_name, m.role, m.is_proxy,
    +            CASE WHEN lower(m.display_name) = lower(p_query) THEN 'exact'
    +                 WHEN m.display_name ILIKE p_pattern ESCAPE E'\\' THEN 'substring'
    +                 ELSE 'fuzzy' END AS match_type,
    +            greatest(similarity(p_query, m.display_name), word_similarity(p_query, m.display_name)) AS score
    +        FROM public.workspace_members m
    +        WHERE m.workspace_id = p_workspace_id
    +          AND m.is_active = true AND m.deleted_at IS NULL
    +          AND char_length(p_query) BETWEEN 2 AND 200
    +          AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.workspaces w WHERE w.id = p_workspace_id AND w.deleted_at IS NULL)
    +          AND (m.display_name ILIKE p_pattern ESCAPE E'\\'
    +               OR (char_length(p_query) >= 3 AND m.display_name %> p_query))
    +    )
    +    SELECT m.id, m.display_name, m.role, m.is_proxy, m.match_type, m.score
    +    FROM matches m
    +    ORDER BY CASE m.match_type WHEN 'exact' THEN 0 WHEN 'substring' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END,
    +             m.score DESC, lower(m.display_name) COLLATE "C", m.id
    +    LIMIT least(21, greatest(2, coalesce(p_limit, 11)));
    +$$;
    +
    +CREATE FUNCTION public.search_containers_ranked(
    +    p_workspace_id uuid, p_query text, p_pattern text, p_limit integer DEFAULT 11
    +) RETURNS TABLE (id uuid, name text, container_type text, status text, match_type text, score real)
    +    LANGUAGE sql STABLE SECURITY INVOKER
    +    SET search_path TO pg_catalog, public, extensions, pg_temp
    +    SET pg_trgm.word_similarity_threshold TO '0.3'
    +    AS $$
    +    WITH matches AS (
    +        SELECT c.id, c.name, c.container_type, c.status,
    +            CASE WHEN lower(c.name) = lower(p_query) THEN 'exact'
    +                 WHEN c.name ILIKE p_pattern ESCAPE E'\\' THEN 'substring'
    +                 ELSE 'fuzzy' END AS match_type,
    +            greatest(similarity(p_query, c.name), word_similarity(p_query, c.name)) AS score
    +        FROM public.containers c
    +        WHERE c.workspace_id = p_workspace_id AND c.deleted_at IS NULL
    +          AND char_length(p_query) BETWEEN 2 AND 200
    +          AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.workspaces w WHERE w.id = p_workspace_id AND w.deleted_at IS NULL)
    +          AND (c.name ILIKE p_pattern ESCAPE E'\\'
    +               OR (char_length(p_query) >= 3 AND c.name %> p_query))
    +    )
    +    SELECT c.id, c.name, c.container_type, c.status, c.match_type, c.score
    +    FROM matches c
    +    ORDER BY CASE c.match_type WHEN 'exact' THEN 0 WHEN 'substring' THEN 1 ELSE 2 END,
    +             c.score DESC, lower(c.name) COLLATE "C", c.id
    +    LIMIT least(21, greatest(2, coalesce(p_limit, 11)));
    +$$;
    +
    +-- These RPCs trust the backend's membership check. Do not let browser roles
    +-- call them with an arbitrary workspace ID, even under Supabase's default
    +-- function grants. Bare local Postgres tests run as the function owner.
    +REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.search_members_ranked(uuid, text, text, integer),
    +    public.search_containers_ranked(uuid, text, text, integer) FROM PUBLIC;
    +
    +DO $$
    +DECLARE browser_role text;
    +BEGIN
    +    FOR browser_role IN SELECT rolname FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname IN ('anon', 'authenticated') LOOP
    +        EXECUTE format('REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.search_members_ranked(uuid, text, text, integer), public.search_containers_ranked(uuid, text, text, integer) FROM %I', browser_role);
    +    END LOOP;
    +    IF EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM pg_roles WHERE rolname = 'service_role') THEN
    +        GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA extensions TO service_role;
    +        GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.search_members_ranked(uuid, text, text, integer),
    +            public.search_containers_ranked(uuid, text, text, integer) TO service_role;
    +    END IF;
    +END;
    +$$;
    +
